With MixChannels > 1 it types one character per frame. [BYTES seg15:0x7e3a seg15:0x7e42 seg15:0x7e50 seg6:0xc9c]
- **Fonts.** Every seg15:0x8174 call that passes the early returns creates big = CreateFont(28, 20, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 2, 0x22, "Arial") and small = CreateFont(22, 13, …same…) and deletes both before it returns. Bytes 0x61–0x7a are drawn uppercased (−0x20) in the small font; every other byte, bytes ≥ 0x80 included because the compare is signed, uses the big font, which gives small capitals. [BYTES seg15:0x8213 seg15:0x823a seg15:0x8446 seg15:0x844a seg15:0x844e seg15:0x8485 seg15:0x849b]

The routine does not reset the alignment; DGROUP:0x13dc is released at the end of each frame ([timing.md](timing.md)). Each drawing call then does SetBkColor(0), SetBkMode(TRANSPARENT), MoveTo(W/2 − Wt/2, H/2 − 150 or H/2 + 100) with Wt = DGROUP:0x1c70, SetTextColor(0x00FFFF00 below 8 bpp, else PALETTERGB(0x00, 0xA5, 0xFF)) and passes each character to seg15:0x7a62, and restores the background and text colours afterwards. - **Emboss.** seg15:0x7a62(char) reads the current position P and draws the one-byte string three times: 0x00FFFFFF at (P.x − 1, P.y − 1), PALETTERGB(0x60, 0x00, 0xC0) at (P.x + 1, P.y + 1), and 0x00FF00FF below 8 bpp (DGROUP:0x90ee < 8) or PALETTERGB(0xA0, 0x60, 0xFF) at P. It then does MoveTo(P.x + GetTextExtent(char).cx, P.y). The colour set at seg15:0x83e9 is always overwritten. [BYTES seg15:0x7a73 seg15:0x7a99 seg15:0x7ab6 seg15:0x7adc seg15:0x7aed seg15:0x7af3 seg15:0x7aff seg15:0x7b11 seg15:0x7b27]
- **Bolt to the cursor.** Each typing call with c ≠ −1 calls seg15:0x80b0(W/2, H/2 − 20, x, y) before it draws, with x = W/2 − Wt/2 + Wt · (c/2) / strlen and y = H/2 − 150 or H/2 + 100; the +10 at seg15:0x835c is dead because the two halvings agree. seg15:0x80b0 finishes any pending bolt (seg15:0x80da), stores (x, y) in DGROUP:0x3b98 and calls seg11:0xa4a6(W/2, H/2 − 20, x, y, 0xe). Full-line redraws draw no bolt. [BYTES seg15:0x830e seg15:0x8318 seg15:0x8344 seg15:0x834e seg15:0x835c seg15:0x837b seg15:0x80bb seg15:0x80c4 seg15:0x80ce]
- **Bolt shape.** seg11:0xa4a6(x1, y1, x2, y2, type) first calls srand(GetCurrentTime()) ([rng.md](rng.md)) and clears the branch counts DGROUP:0x9526 and DGROUP:0x9528. With dx = x2 − x1, dy = y2 − y1 and g = max(|dx| + |dy|, 3) it takes L = ((dx² + dy²)/g + g)/2 (one Newton step, seg8:0x1290). The segment length S is 30, or 50 when L/30 > 22; when L/50 > 22 it returns without drawing. N = L/S. The main points DGROUP:0x6b5c[i] are P0 = (x1, y1), P_N = (x2, y2) and, for 0 < i < N, x = x1 + ((rand%10 + (i+1)S − 5)·dx)/L + ((rand%26 − 13)·(−dy))/L and y = y1 + ((rand%10 + (i+1)S − 5)·dy)/L + ((rand%26 − 13)·dx)/L, with the rand calls in that order and each product divided separately. [BYTES seg11:0xa4bb seg11:0xa4c3 seg11:0xa4c9 seg11:0xa4d4 seg11:0xa4e2 seg11:0xa529 seg11:0xa537 seg11:0xa53c seg11:0xa54d seg11:0xa559 seg11:0xa7d5 seg11:0xa83a seg11:0xa884 seg11:0xa897 seg8:0x1290]
  - After each main point P_i (i = 0 … N − 1), rand()%3 = 0 adds branch b (DGROUP:0x6bbc + 20b, 5 points): point 0 = P_i and, for k = 1–4 with p = (32·(i%2) − 16)·k, x = P_i.x + ((rand%24 + p − 12)·(−dy))/L + ((rand%15 + 15k)·dx)/L, y = P_i.y + ((rand%24 + p − 12)·dx)/L + ((rand%15 + 15k)·dy)/L. More than 16 branches aborts the whole bolt with nothing drawn. Then rand()%5 ≤ 3 adds sub-branch s (DGROUP:0x6cfc + 12s, 3 points) from branch point 2 (Q): for m = 1, 2 with q = 12·(2(m+2)·(i%2) − m), x = Q.x + ((rand%16 + q − 32)·(−dy))/L + ((rand%15 + 15(m+2))·dx)/L, y = Q.y + ((rand%16 + q − 32)·dx)/L + ((rand%15 + 15(m+2))·dy)/L. [BYTES seg11:0xa561 seg11:0xa56e seg11:0xa585 seg11:0xa5c6 seg11:0xa614 seg11:0xa66c seg11:0xa67b seg11:0xa684 seg11:0xa696 seg11:0xa6ac seg11:0xa752 seg11:0xa7a9 seg11:0xa7b8]
  - It draws at once with the clipped line routine seg12:0x7256: the main polyline P0 … PN with CreatePen(PS_SOLID, 2, 0x00FFFFC3 = RGB(0xC3, 0xFF, 0xFF)), or 0x0000FF00 for type 9, and every branch (4 segments) and sub-branch (2 segments) with a 1-px pen of the same colour. It then sets DGROUP:0x1016 = N + 1. [BYTES seg11:0xa8a3 seg11:0xa8bc seg11:0xa8c1 seg11:0xa8ea seg11:0xa932 seg11:0xa968 seg11:0xa9ac seg11:0xa9de]
- **Bolt finish.** seg15:0x80da does nothing while DGROUP:0x1016 = 0. With Calibrate < 1400 it makes one seg11:0xa9ea(0) call and sets DGROUP:0x1016 = 0; otherwise it calls seg11:0xa9ea(0) until DGROUP:0x1016 = 0. seg11:0xa9ea sets OPAQUE. When DGROUP:0x1016 > 0 it redraws the main polyline with a black 2-px pen and the branches with a black 1-px pen; if DGROUP:0x1036 = 0 it then redraws only the branches and sub-branches again with stock BLACK_PEN and negates DGROUP:0x1016. When DGROUP:0x1016 < 0 it redraws everything with RGB(0x7F, 0xFF, 0xFF) pens (2 px main, 1 px branches), then sets DGROUP:0x1036 = 1 and negates if DGROUP:0x1036 was 0, else clears both. A positive value with DGROUP:0x1036 ≠ 0 draws black and clears both. From DGROUP:0x1036 = 0 on the port's path, one seg15:0x80da call erases the bolt, flashes it in RGB(0x7F, 0xFF, 0xFF) and erases it again, ending with DGROUP:0x1016 = DGROUP:0x1036 = 0. Each bolt stays visible until the next seg15:0x80da. [BYTES seg15:0x80e4 seg15:0x80eb seg15:0x80f5 seg15:0x80fb seg15:0x8105 seg15:0x810b seg11:0xa9fd seg11:0xaa02 seg11:0xaa65 seg11:0xaaf9 seg11:0xabb0 seg11:0xabbf seg11:0xac61 seg11:0xac6e seg11:0xac78]
- **Erase.** Once DGROUP:0x6e2c (name completion + 3000 ms) < now the entry is erased within one frame. k = 1 when MixChannels DGROUP:0x920a = 1, else 4. The top line gets k times: x = rand()%300 + W/2 − 150, seg15:0x80b0(W/2, H/2 − 20, x, H/2 − 150), sound seg10:0x754a(0x22, x, H/2 − 150, 4) and a 40 ms busy wait on GetCurrentTime; then seg15:0x80da, PatBlt(0, H/2 − 150, W, 40, BLACKNESS), the logo StretchBlt again and a 270 ms busy wait. The bottom line gets the same k bolts to (rand()%300 + W/2 − 150, H/2 + 100), then seg15:0x80da and PatBlt(0, H/2 + 100, W, 40, BLACKNESS). It then sets DGROUP:0x1e50 = now and n = DGROUP:0x95d8 + 1. For n ≤ 5, DGROUP:0x130c = now + 1000: one second of logo alone, then seg15:0x811a. For n > 5, DGROUP:0x6e28 = now + 4000 and DGROUP:0x6e2c = now + 9000, so after 4000 ms of logo alone the phase ends with the normal transition (sound 0x6e, black, phase 4). [BYTES seg15:0x7e7b seg15:0x7ea1 seg15:0x7ec4 seg15:0x7edf seg15:0x7eee seg15:0x7efb seg15:0x7f24 seg15:0x7f43 seg15:0x7f9e seg15:0x7fa8 seg15:0x7fcd seg15:0x7ffe seg15:0x8043 seg15:0x8062 seg15:0x806c seg15:0x8073 seg15:0x8077 seg15:0x807e seg15:0x8095 seg15:0x809b]
- **Timeline on the port's path.** 83 frames of logo growth come alone; the 84th clamps DGROUP:0x95d0 to 300 and already makes the first typing call (c = 0, a bolt and sound 0x14). Each entry then takes floor(L1/2) + 2 frames for the heading and floor(L2/2) + 2 for the name (the last frame of each is the completion redraw, with no bolt or sound), a 3000 ms hold, a blocking erase of about 40 + 270 + 40 ms plus drawing, and for entries 0–4 a 1000 ms logo-only gap followed by one reload frame that does not type. After entry 5 comes a 4000 ms logo-only tail. Each reload sets the deadline to now + 90000 ms, so that deadline never ends the phase. The Calibrate tests on this path are seg15:0x7cef and seg15:0x7d10 (< 800), seg15:0x7e3a (> 900, with MixChannels > 1 at seg15:0x7e42) and seg15:0x80eb (< 1400). [BYTES seg15:0x7d2c seg15:0x7da8 seg15:0x7e1c seg15:0x7efb seg15:0x7fa8 seg15:0x807e seg15:0x809b seg15:0x8169 seg15:0x7cef seg15:0x7d10 seg15:0x7e3a seg15:0x7e42 seg15:0x80eb] [DESIGN]
- **Skipped runs.** Because the entry resets neither DGROUP:0x95da, DGROUP:0x130c nor DGROUP:0x1c70 (written only at seg15:0x8276 and seg15:0x82fc, when c/2 = 0), a run skipped with Space in the middle of typing leaves the next run's first heading call with c/2 > 0: the heading skips the width measurement, is placed and aimed with the previous line's width, and appears partly typed, or completes at once when the left-over c/2 exceeds its length. A run skipped in the 1000 ms gap makes the next run reload entry 0 again on its first text frame, which costs one frame. [BYTES seg15:0x7b34 seg15:0x7e31 seg15:0x80a1 seg15:0x7dcb seg15:0x8243 seg15:0x8276 seg15:0x82fc seg15:0x833c seg15:0x83a5]

**Repaint.** A repaint in state 0 (seg11:0xafe case 0) always ends with seg15:0x5962(1). Before that, phase 0 changes nothing. Phases 1 and 2 set the deadline to now, so the repaint ends the black second or the title. Phases 3–5 move a deadline that is not already DGROUP:0x1e50 to now + 15000 ms. Phases 6 and 7 set DGROUP:0x92d4 = 9 when it is ≥ 2, else copy the desk bitmap DC DGROUP:0x929a to the screen. [BYTES seg11:0x15fc seg11:0x1603 seg11:0x160a seg11:0x162f seg11:0x1636 seg11:0x163b seg11:0x1642 seg11:0x164b seg11:0x1661 seg11:0x166a]

**Input.**
- The tick's state-0 case passes the pending message to seg11:0x7902 with the raw wParam. Only input that arrives during a frame goes through the router seg11:0x6aa8, which remaps key codes through seg11:0xb306. [BYTES seg7:0x21e seg7:0x22b seg2:0x77b seg2:0x79d seg11:0x6aff]
- No key reaches the state-0 handler before the first Calibrate measurement ends. Outside DEMO the input path needs DGROUP:0x1212 ≠ 0, and seg15:0x6009 sets DGROUP:0x1212 only when the measurement is accepted. Skipping the intro therefore never skips Calibrate, and seg6:0xa6c has called seg6:0xb3e (FrameRateSet) before any game starts. The input path also drops keys until DGROUP:0x3b94, which the dialog wrappers set to now + 500 ms after each dialog. [BYTES seg2:0x74a seg2:0x75c seg15:0x6009 seg15:0x6030 seg10:0x8401 seg10:0x8624]
- The state-0 input handler seg11:0x7902 acts only on WM_KEYUP (0x101). It first clears DGROUP:0x90f8 when it is set with DGROUP:0x8e5e = 0 (PLAY). It handles exactly the key codes below; every other key, Return included, does nothing. [BYTES seg11:0x7918 seg11:0x7b1b seg11:0x791e seg11:0x792c seg11:0x7935 seg11:0x793e seg11:0x7944 seg11:0x794a seg11:0x794e seg11:0x7952]

  | Key | Action | Evidence |
  |---|---|---|
  | S | sounds 0x6e and 0x13, New Demo through seg10:0x82a2(4); on a non-zero result deletes demogame.sav (seg6:0x92b8) and continues as D | [BYTES seg11:0x7959 seg11:0x7961 seg11:0x796b seg11:0x7975] |
  | D | seg10:0x38e0, then continues as C | [BYTES seg11:0x797a seg11:0x7981] |
  | C | stores seg10:0x804c(1) and seg10:0x804c(0) in DGROUP:0x939a and DGROUP:0x939e; when the phase is below 6 and DGROUP:0x120e = 0, sets the deadline to now, the phase to 5 and DGROUP:0x9516 to 1 for D or 0 otherwise. The next frame then takes the demo-start path, which restores demogame.sav only while DGROUP:0x9516 = 0 | [BYTES seg11:0x7990 seg11:0x799d seg11:0x79a4 seg11:0x79b3 seg11:0x79b7 seg11:0x79bd seg11:0x79c3 seg11:0x79cc] |
  | R | only while DGROUP:0x920c is 0 or 1: sound 0x51 and seg10:0x80a8, which opens the Order dialog (seg10:0x82a2(8)) at most 20 times per run (counter DGROUP:0x921e) and returns its result; a non-zero result runs seg10:0x885e(0) | [BYTES seg11:0x79d5 seg11:0x79dc seg11:0x79e5 seg11:0x79eb seg11:0x79f9 seg10:0x80b5 seg10:0x80bc seg10:0x80c7] |
  | ESC | the game-start flow ([Game-start dialogs](#game-start-dialogs)) | [BYTES seg11:0x7a02] |
  | Space | skips the current phase (deadline = now) when the phase is below 6 and DGROUP:0x120e = 0; otherwise, when DGROUP:0x92d4 = 1, calls seg15:0x1584(1) (desk-scene typing) | [BYTES seg11:0x7aea seg11:0x7af1 seg11:0x7af9 seg11:0x7afd seg11:0x7b03 seg11:0x7b0a seg11:0x7b13] |

  - Space during the title scene: on the next frame the title routine runs first and, since deadline − 200 < now, seg15:0x7962 sets DGROUP:0x94c4 = 1; the dispatcher then plays sound 0x6e, blacks the screen and enters the credits. [BYTES seg11:0x7aea seg15:0x7962 seg15:0x7840 seg15:0x59df]
  - seg10:0x80a8 returns its uninitialised local [bp−4] once the Order counter DGROUP:0x921e has reached 20, so a later 'R' can still run seg10:0x885e(0) at random. [BYTES seg10:0x80ba seg10:0x80d2 seg11:0x79f0] A port returns 0 there. [DESIGN]
- **Mouse.** A left click does nothing in state 0: WM_LBUTTONDOWN goes to seg16:0x638a, which returns unless DGROUP:0x95bc ≠ 0 and DGROUP:0x952c ≠ 0 (0 in the probe's title dump), and no mouse message reaches seg11:0x7902. A right click, once DGROUP:0x1212 ≠ 0, opens the popup menu ENG_LANG.DLL MENU 255 through seg2:0x134c, whose commands go to the WM_COMMAND handler. A double click goes to seg2:0x1046 when DGROUP:0x1212 ≠ 0 and DGROUP:0x22 ≠ 0. [BYTES seg16:0x6397 seg16:0x63aa seg2:0x683 seg2:0x68e seg2:0x1361 seg2:0x139b seg2:0x65e] [ORACLE probe-0001]

## Game-start dialogs
**Ordinal 10.** seg10:0x82a2(cmd) is the wrapper of ordinal 10 for every command except 0 and 3. seg10:0x7f18 issues command 0 (caller seg2:0x14ca) and seg10:0x8568 command 3 (callers seg2:0x55e, seg2:0xe5d, seg2:0x1500). INSPACE never issues commands 2, 10 and 13, and issues 9 only from the cmd-10 branch, which has no caller. [BYTES seg2:0x548 seg2:0xe0f seg2:0xe35 seg11:0x796b seg11:0x7a43 seg11:0x7a7c seg16:0x1365 seg16:0x4811 seg10:0x80c7 seg10:0x882d seg10:0x834d seg10:0x7f4e seg10:0x8606 seg10:0x8332 seg2:0x14ca seg2:0x55e seg2:0xe5d seg2:0x1500]

| Command | Caller | Dialog or purpose | x4 | x5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | seg10:0x7f18 | Options ([timing.md](timing.md#rate-settings)) | | |
| 1 | seg11:0x7a7c (ESC) | Restore Game #267, then New Game #321 | 0 | 1 when DGROUP:0x920c = 0, 3 when it is 3, else 0 |
| 3 | seg10:0x8568 | Save Game #00a7 | | |
| 4 | seg11:0x796b (S) | New Demo #589 | 0 | as command 1 |
| 5 | seg2:0xe0f (SC_CLOSE) | exit dialog #244 | DGROUP:0x90f4 | DGROUP:0x95bc |
| 6 | seg16:0x1365, and seg16:0x4811 as −100 with hwnd DGROUP:0x9532 | ambulance options | DGROUP:0x95bc | as command 1 |
| 7 | seg2:0x548 (0x991) | exit dialog #244 | DGROUP:0x90f4 | DGROUP:0x95bc |
| 8 | seg10:0x80c7 ('R') | Order | DGROUP:0x9224 when DGROUP:0x922a = 0, else 0 | date number |
| 11 | seg11:0x7a43 (ESC) | Important Information | 0 | 0 |
| 12 | seg2:0xe35 (Exit) | Registration Benefits #799 | 0 | 0 |
| 14 | seg10:0x882d | #1872 | 0 | 0 |
| 15 | seg10:0x82a2 itself, before 8 | date (tm_mday, tm_mon + 1, tm_year) | | |

[BYTES seg10:0x82c6 seg10:0x8300 seg10:0x8314 seg10:0x836c seg10:0x8372 seg10:0x8380 seg10:0x83b7 seg10:0x83b0 seg10:0x83da seg10:0x83e3] [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg9:0x1468 seg9:0x1484 seg9:0x1492] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL DIALOG 267] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL DIALOG 321] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL DIALOG 244] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL DIALOG 799] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL DIALOG 1872] [ORACLE wave-0001]

- seg10:0x82a2 calls ordinal 10 at seg10:0x83e3 with (hwnd, DGROUP:0x39f2, DGROUP:0x920e, x4, x5, 0, cmd), hwnd being DGROUP:0x16, or DGROUP:0x9532 for −100. The date number is (tm_year − 93) × 365 + tm_yday. For 8 (and 10) it first sends command 15 with (hPal, x4, x5) = (tm_mday, tm_mon + 1, tm_year); command 10 never reaches seg10:0x83e3. [BYTES seg10:0x82c6 seg10:0x8300 seg10:0x8314 seg10:0x83da seg10:0x83e3]
- **Around the dialog** seg10:0x82a2 calls seg10:0x7480(−2, 1) before it (except for command 10). After it, it resets DGROUP:0x1e50 to now, sets DGROUP:0x3b94 = now + 500 ms and calls seg10:0x7f88. [BYTES seg10:0x82bb seg10:0x83ef seg10:0x8401 seg10:0x8409 seg10:0x841d seg10:0x8429 seg10:0x8438 seg10:0x843d seg10:0x853d seg10:0x8550 seg10:0x844a seg10:0x845b seg10:0x847d seg10:0x8496 seg10:0x84ee seg10:0x84fc seg10:0x8505 seg10:0x8531 seg10:0x8534]
  - Command 12 then returns its result at once, without seg10:0x7480(−3, 1). A non-zero result first plays sound 0x13 and opens Order through seg10:0x80a8.
  - Every other command ends with seg10:0x7480(−3, 1). Before that, a zero result of commands other than 5 and 6 sets the phase deadline to now (phase below 4) or now + 15000 ms.
  - A non-zero result clears DGROUP:0x8f64, sets the deadline to now + 40000 ms, clears DGROUP:0x104e, DGROUP:0x104c, DGROUP:0x951c and DGROUP:0x9516 and stores the INI Drive letter minus 'C' in DGROUP:0x94ac. For command 1 it also reads PlayMode into DGROUP:0x9134, ThePlayer into DGROUP:0x380c and, when Restore contains a space, stores seg10:0x804c(1) and (0) in DGROUP:0x939a and DGROUP:0x939e.
- seg10:0x804c(1) returns time(). seg10:0x804c(0) returns the local date packed as DX = tm_year and AX = tm_mday \| (tm_mon << 8). [BYTES seg10:0x805d seg10:0x8069 seg10:0x8075 seg10:0x8080 seg10:0x8088 seg10:0x808c seg10:0x8091]
- **Music around dialogs.** seg10:0x7480(code, arg) is the music selector. Codes 9 and 10 are ignored, with no ISPACE call, while track 9 or 10 plays, and 10 becomes 9 while track 1 plays. Code −2 (before every ordinal-10 dialog of seg10:0x82a2 except command 10, before command 0 at seg10:0x7f31 and in seg10:0x8568) saves DGROUP:0x9220 into the byte DGROUP:0x7e0[DGROUP:0x73e] and sets DGROUP:0x9222 = −2. Every other code sets DGROUP:0x9222 = −3, and −3 is replaced by the saved byte (sign-extended) when that differs from DGROUP:0x9220. A resulting code ≥ 0 is stored in DGROUP:0x9220 (7 stored as 2), and a music deadline DGROUP:0x2384 below DGROUP:0x1e50 (unsigned) becomes DGROUP:0x1e50 + 9000. With DGROUP:0x9210 ≠ 0 it then calls ISPACE ordinal 7 command 11 with (code, arg), code being the value before the 7 → 2 mapping. seg10:0x82a2 issues −3 after every dialog except commands 10 and 12. [BYTES seg10:0x748d seg10:0x74aa seg10:0x74bc seg10:0x74c9 seg10:0x74cd seg10:0x74d5 seg10:0x74e5 seg10:0x74f0 seg10:0x74ff seg10:0x7512 seg10:0x751d seg10:0x752a seg10:0x7531 seg10:0x753e seg10:0x82bb seg10:0x841d seg10:0x842f seg10:0x8679]
- seg10:0x7f88 runs after every ordinal-10 dialog of seg10:0x82a2 (command 10 returns earlier) and from seg10:0x7f58 and seg7:0xb7a. It rereads INI Size and applies a change ([Window geometry](#window-geometry), **Keys**), which is how New Game's ZOOM LEVEL and Options' Zoom Level take effect. seg10:0x8568's Save Game call does not run it.   - **Serial.** ISPACE.DLL seg10:0x5dfe(hDlg, bump) reads the 10-byte IGRAPHIC.DLL #5500 110 record ([formats.md](formats.md#resources-rewritten-in-place)) and loads its serial into ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0x154c. When the counter (+4, signed) is above 8 or the serial (+6) is 0, it calls seg10:0x492(hDlg), stores DGROUP:0x154c at +6, sets +4 = 0 and +0 = seg10:0x4440(DGROUP:0x2056[0] − 0x40); otherwise a non-zero bump adds 1 to +4. It writes the record back with seg10:0x4636. When the read or the write returns 0 it calls seg10:0x492(hDlg) for a fresh serial that is not saved; otherwise it writes ltoa(DGROUP:0x154c, 10) into DGROUP:0x1674, sets it as the text of control 700 and focuses control 701. It returns the read or write result. The Order init passes bump 0 (seg10:0x74) and a wrong code bump 1 (seg10:0x93d, after MessageBox(string 2271, MB_ICONHAND)), so nine wrong codes leave the counter at 9 and the next call, an opening of Order or the tenth wrong code, shows a new serial. Each of the n iterations computes v afresh: v = (rand() % 5 + 1)·2931 + (rand() % 32)·4 + (rand() % 50)·17 + rand() % 321 + rand() % 1242, each term 16-bit and sign-extended; v += D·(rand() % 17 + 813) with D = ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0x213a sign-extended (the date number, x5 of ordinal 10 command 8); when v < 21400 (signed), v += (rand() % 4 + 2)·1234 and then rand() % 1010; v = labs(v) (seg2:0x824); when 600000 < v < 700000, v −= 500000; and always v += (rand() % 6)·100000 (seg3:0x53c). After the loop a non-zero hDlg gets ltoa(v, 10) (seg3:0x69c) in DGROUP:0x1674, as the text of control 700, and the focus on control 701. **From ESC to the wave.** The path from the title to a wave runs as follows.

1. **ESC** plays sound 0x6e. In phase 6 it sets DGROUP:0x92d4 = 9 (the desk scene jumps to its last step). Otherwise it reads INI Accept. DGROUP:0x920c = 2 with Accept ≠ 42 opens Important Information (seg10:0x82a2(11)). It does nothing in state 1, while DGROUP:0x120e ≠ 0, while DGROUP:0x1212 = 0 and in phase 7. Below phase 6 it clears DGROUP:0x92e4, plays sound 0x13 and opens Restore Game through seg10:0x82a2(1). The item is "C: " (DGROUP:0x32b) with the letter raised to 'A' + d − 1, then the label, added with CB_ADDSTRING (0x403); the selection is the item whose letter equals the INI Drive's first byte (a case-sensitive byte compare). At 1024 × 768 the play height is 683, so k = −190, the pad is 159 wide, DGROUP:0x3c9a = 74 and the bitmap is 159 × 126. seg15:0x9e6 makes 16 perspective frames of the ship image (DGROUP:0x3ccc, mask DGROUP:0x3cce) from the INI MainShip design (default EAGLE), which seg15:0x2a08 renders through seg10:0x73a6.      - **The pad's geometry.** All of seg15:0x482e's arithmetic is signed 16-bit, half(v) the `cwd; sub ax, dx; sar ax, 1` halving toward zero. With q = trunc(lo16(DGROUP:0x90ec << 2)/3) and k = q − 1100 it stores the top width A = DGROUP:0x3ce4 = trunc(k/22) + 139, the width pw = DGROUP:0x3ce6 = trunc(k/36) + 164 and DGROUP:0x92dc = 200. The eight words DGROUP:0x3c8c … 0x3c9b are the trapezoid's four POINTs: P0 = (half(pw) − half(A), 1), P1 = (half(pw) + half(A), 1), P2 = (pw, pc) and P3 = (0, pc), with pc = trunc(k/30) + 80 at DGROUP:0x3c96 and DGROUP:0x3c9a. It stores X = DGROUP:0x3c9c = half(q) − half(pw) and DGROUP:0x3c9e = 0. With the slopes m1 = trunc(lo16(100·(1 − pc))/P0.x) and m2 = trunc(lo16(100·(1 − pc))/(P1.x − pw)) it stores the sloped edges' crossing, DGROUP:0x3ca0 = lo16((X·(m1 − m2) − pw·m2)/(m1 − m2)) and DGROUP:0x3ca2 = lo16(m1·(DGROUP:0x3ca0 − X)/100) + DGROUP:0x10 − 52, both through the 32-bit helpers seg5:0x314 and seg5:0x110. At 1024 × 768 (play height 683, client 768): q 910, A 131, pw 159, pc 74, P0 (14, 1), P1 (144, 1), m1 −521, m2 486, X 376, DGROUP:0x3ca0 = 452 and DGROUP:0x3ca2 = 321. MERGECOPY takes the mono mask's 1 to d's background colour, white, so each band's grey lands inside the trapezoid and black outside it. [BYTES seg15:0x4aa6-0x4b46] [REF Win16 SDK BitBlt]

       - **Branch A**, while DGROUP:0x92b0 does not exist: GetPrivateProfileString(MainShip, default "EAGLE") into a 20-byte buffer, so at most 19 characters, and the search i = 0, 1, … while i < DGROUP:0x95aa for the first lstrcmp(SHP + 0x41·i + 0x0a, buffer) = 0. The code after the search reads record i whether or not it matched: a MainShip that matches nothing reads record DGROUP:0x95aa, one past the table, which the port reads as a zeroed record ([ADR 0016](../decisions/0016-undefined-reads.md) decision 2). With BOD = DGROUP:0x6e1c + 0x2c·(SHP word +0x21): the wings flag [bp−0x42] = BOD byte +0x20 & 4 (Pull-off Wings), fy = sext(BOD byte +0x19) (the flame's y), flame on [bp−0x3e] = 1 when BOD +0x20 bit 0 is clear, the flame row [bp−0x3c] = 1 when SHP byte +0x09 bit 0 is clear, the behind flag [bp−0x40] = BOD +0x20 & 8 and the band [bp−0x16] = sext(BOD byte +0x1a). DGROUP:0x92b0 = seg10:0x73a6(BOD word +0x21, the lstrcpy of BOD +0x23, −2, −2, 2, 0, 1, SHP +0x07, SHP +0x08), which is ordinal 12 at twice the body height ([ispace.md](ispace.md#ordinal-notes)). Then s = trunc(bmHeight/k), w8 = half(s − band), DGROUP:0x92b2 = CreateCompatibleBitmap(DGROUP:0x13dc, lo16(k·s), lo16(k·s)) and, with `Size` DGROUP:0x9156 set to 1 around the call, the flame DGROUP:0x3cc8 = seg10:0x7032(1, 1, row), ordinal 18 with x = 1, with its mask DGROUP:0x3cca = seg10:0x6ec6(DGROUP:0x3cc8). Frame i = 1 … 15 has w_i = w_(i−1) − step and h_i = trunc(lo16(w_i·p_i)/100), the product's high word replaced by `cwd`, with p_i = 95, 89, … 47 (down 6) for i = 1 … 9 and 41, 54, … 106 (up 13) for i = 10 … 15.    - **Formatter.** seg10:0xa332(dst, id, overwrite, ...) loads string id from DGROUP:0x9212. overwrite ≠ 0 writes from dst[0], and 0 appends at lstrlen(dst). %< takes the next vararg as the output flag [bp−0x138], which starts at 1; while the flag is 0 nothing is written, though %d, %#, %S, %A, %V, %R and %D still take their arguments. A non-zero flag makes the formatter scan forward to the next %< or %> marker: when an earlier enabled group of the run exists (counter [bp−0xa] > 0) it writes ", " for a %< or string 797 " AND" and a space for a %>, and either way it raises the counter; a scan that reaches the end of the format writes nothing and leaves the counter. %> sets the flag to 1, skips its two characters and resets the counter to 0.    - **Other directives.** %d prints its one argument as a signed 16-bit decimal (seg6:0xf1a) and %l a dword as a signed 32-bit decimal (seg6:0xeb8). %S, %A and %V load the next argument as a string id into an 80-byte buffer and cut the string at its second-to-last character when that is '^', so a trailing caret pair such as ^N goes; 46 ENG_LANG.DLL strings end in such a pair and none in a bare '^'. %N and %n append CR LF whenever the flag is on: their phase-11 test also needs the byte [bp−0x6a] to be 0, and that byte holds the directive letter. %R draws its `rand()` even while the flag is 0, and its string gets no caret cut ([rng.md](rng.md)). In the format string itself ^P, ^F, ^M and ^N are dropped with their letter and any other caret is copied; an unknown directive skips three characters. After the TextOut x advances by the character's GetTextExtent, a CreateSolidBrush(0x0000FF80) PATCOPY block of the same size goes to (new x + 2, y + h − 10h/13), and (x, y) is saved in DGROUP:0x17e6/0x17e8.    - At a space the routine copies the next word and its delimiter into buf[1 …] and measures GetTextExtent from buf = [bp − 0x5c], which covers a never-written byte and the word without its delimiter; it wraps when DGROUP:0x6088 − 9 < x + extent. A CR or a wrap increments DGROUP:0x17ec and resets x to DGROUP:0x3d48 + 29; a CR also consumes the following LF, a wrap only the space. While DGROUP:0x17ec < (DGROUP:0x92d2 − 58)/(h + 1) it adds h + 1 to y (DGROUP:0x17e4); otherwise it scrolls DGROUP:0x6084 with ScrollDC(0, −1) h + 1 times and subtracts h + 1 from the cursor y DGROUP:0x17e8. Any byte below 13 as a signed value, except NUL and LF, becomes CR, bytes 0x80–0xff included. Typing raises the step at a CR whose index + 2 holds a space (1 → 2) and at the NUL (4 → 5, also setting DGROUP:0x92a = 1). Space at step 1 calls seg15:0x1584(1), which sets step 2 and moves the index to that CR, skipping to the launch. 9. **Launch animation.** Step 5 BitBlts the monitor text area (DGROUP:0x3ca4 + 29, DGROUP:0x3ca6 + 29, width − 58, DGROUP:0x92d2 − 58) from the screen into the scene DC, busy-waits 800 ms on GetCurrentTime and calls seg15:0x0(2), which plays sound 0x20; the deadline becomes now + 950. Step 6 waits for it and calls seg15:0x0(1) (sound 0x1c), and step 7 repeats seg15:0x0(0). seg15:0x0 moves the ship up from y0 = DGROUP:0x92d8 = H − s4 − 62 to ye = DGROUP:0x92d6 = 60, keeping the position in tenths, pos10 (DGROUP:0x17c2, starting at 10·y0). The frame f (0–15) indexes the 16 ship frames. In state 0, s4 is 4·DGROUP:0x92da, the size that seg15:0x2a08 returns. [BYTES seg15:0x1193 seg15:0x58 seg15:0xc3 seg15:0xce seg15:0x14f seg15:0x185 seg15:0x219 seg15:0x2c4 seg15:0x598 seg15:0xa35]
   - **Step rule.** With R = y0 − ye, the frame is f = (y0 − trunc(pos10/10)) / max(1, trunc(trunc(R/2)/10)) while the previous frame DGROUP:0x17ca is below 10. From then on it is f = (trunc((ye − y0)/2) − trunc(pos10/10) + y0) / max(1, trunc(trunc(R/2)/6)) + 10, capped at 15. [BYTES seg15:0x18b seg15:0x1b6 seg15:0x1c9 seg15:0x1ef seg15:0x212 seg15:0x216 seg15:0x21c]
     - The speed DGROUP:0x17c8 is max(9, 150 − 4f) when Calibrate DGROUP:0x8ffc ≥ 1400 and max(9, 150 − trunc(f/2)) otherwise. After drawing, pos10 += trunc(3·speed/−7) at Calibrate ≥ 1400, or trunc(5·speed/−7) below. The call returns 1 once 10·ye > pos10. [BYTES seg15:0x2c4 seg15:0x2dc seg15:0x2e4 seg15:0x598 seg15:0x5ac seg15:0x5b2 seg15:0x5b6]
     - Calibrate only selects the speed falloff and the 3/7 or 5/7 factor. For y0 = 574 the rise takes 108 calls at Calibrate ≥ 1400, in steps of about 6 px falling to 4, and 50 calls below 1400, in steps of about 11 and then 10 px; both counts include the step-5 and step-6 calls. [BYTES seg15:0x2c4 seg15:0x598]
   - **Rate.** Case 7 of the desk switch (seg15:0x1245–0x1258) calls seg15:0x0(0) with no GetCurrentTime test, once per desk-frame pass; only steps 5 and 6 are timed (the 800 ms wait, then the deadline now + 950 at seg15:0x121e–0x1232). The oracle shows the rise from t064.17 to t065.54, about 106 calls in 1.37 s, roughly 12.8 ms per call. What paces the desk-frame pass is [OPEN] ([Open questions](#open-questions)). [BYTES seg15:0x1245 seg15:0x1248 seg15:0x1258 seg15:0x121e seg15:0x1232] [ORACLE start-0001 main]
   - **Drawing.** Each call draws into the work DC DGROUP:0x92b8 that seg15:0x3bfa(4s, 4s + 15) makes at the first call. seg15:0x3bfa(w, h) frees an existing work DC (restoring DGROUP:0x92ae and the palette DGROUP:0x92c2, deleting DGROUP:0x92ac, both zeroed) and makes DGROUP:0x92b8 = CreateCompatibleDC(DGROUP:0x13dc) with DGROUP:0x92ac = CreateCompatibleBitmap(DGROUP:0x13dc, w, h), DGROUP:0x920e selected (old palette DGROUP:0x92c2) and a BLACKNESS PatBlt, nothing more. The steps are: [BYTES seg15:0x163 seg15:0x3c04-0x3c9e]
     1. PatBlt BLACKNESS over the work DC.
     2. In desk phase DGROUP:0x95b8 = 6, or in state 8, the part of the ship rectangle inside (slot 0 left, slot 0 top, slot 0 left + slot 0 width, DGROUP:0x92de) is copied from the scene DC DGROUP:0x929a at the same screen coordinates, when IntersectRect finds a part. In state 6 the work DC holds black, the pad part and the frame.
     3. The part inside the pad rectangle is copied from the pad DC DGROUP:0x92b6.
     4. At 8 bpp or less only, the shadow: with risen = y0 − trunc(pos10/10), state ≠ 0 skips it once risen ≥ 80; in state 0, d = trunc(pos10/10) − ye replaces risen when below 40, and risen is capped at 40. off = trunc(risen/3) + 4, step = DGROUP:0x17cc − DGROUP:0x17dc (the previous top minus this one) and hh = min(h0 − step − off + 14, h_f); under DGROUP:0x5ea8 (below 8 bpp) or DGROUP:0x5eaa it does BitBlt(work, half(w0) − half(w_f), off, w_f, hh, DGROUP:0x3cde, 0, y_f, 0x00A803A9). ROP 0xA8 is D AND (P OR S), so the frame's mask keeps D outside the ship and the checkerboard brush blacks every other pixel of the ship's silhouette: a shadow the 24-bpp oracle never draws ([ADR 0007](../decisions/0007-palette.md)). [BYTES seg15:0x3d9-0x4d1 seg15:0x2be seg15:0x592] [REF Win16 SDK ternary raster operations]
     5. Frame f is drawn with an SRCAND mask and an SRCPAINT image at (2s − trunc(w_f/2), 0), top-aligned. For odd w_f this is one pixel right of trunc((4s − w_f)/2).
     6. The 4s × (4s + 15) work DC is copied to the screen with BitBlt at (trunc(W/2) − 2s, trunc(pos10/10)).

It returns 1 when 10·DGROUP:0x92d6 > DGROUP:0x17c2. On the way out in state 0 it copies the monitor text area back from the scene DC to the screen and plays seg10:0x754a(0x4c, 0, 0, 0). With WHITE_PEN it draws a cross of half-length i/10 for i = 2 … 199 (up to 19 px), then erases it with black SetPixel at the four end points for i = 200 down to 1, each time also PatBlt BLACKNESS 2×2 at (x − 1, y − 1). Every lit pixel goes back to black, not to the previous background, inside the one call, so the flash leaves nothing behind. The briefing's two typers call it on their resets. [BYTES seg15:0x84bf seg15:0x84cd seg15:0x84d5 seg15:0x856b seg15:0x85f7 seg9:0x8d60 seg17:0x2c87]

Between t065.54 and t066.97 Wine flushed nothing to the screen, so the ship appears to stay until t066.97, when the second restore shows. start-0001 main ran with SoundSystem=0, so ordinal 9 returned at ISPACE.DLL seg9:0xc57 and 0x4c did not play. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg9:0xc50 seg9:0xc57] Nothing in the bytes hides the ship at the monitor neck. [BYTES seg15:0x5c4 seg15:0x6db seg15:0x74f] [ORACLE start-0001 main]

So the first restore, 0x4c, the flash and the second restore run back to back, as the bytes order them, and the hold is the pump-wait alone. ## The landing scene
States 6, 8 and 9 open with the landing scene seg15:0x302e; this section follows state 6, the ambulance visit, whose phases are in [core.md](core.md#the-visit-state-6-seg160xcfe). Phase 0 calls seg15:0x302e once; phase 1's seg15:0x3590 then runs the incoming jet seg15:0x3caa on its sub-step 0, the descent's set-up at its end and the descent seg15:0x374c ([core.md](core.md#the-descent-seg150x374c)) on its sub-step 1. None of seg15:0x302e, seg15:0x3caa and seg15:0x3590 references DGROUP:0x920c, so the corridor and the jet are the same in both editions; the editions differ in the visit only in the left monitor's text ([core.md](core.md#the-screen)). [BYTES seg16:0xd9b seg16:0xdb9 seg15:0x302e-0x358d seg15:0x3590-0x374a seg15:0x3caa-0x454a]
- **seg15:0x302e in state 6:**
  1. Sound 0x6e. With DGROUP:0x93a6 = 0, PatBlt(DGROUP:0x13dc, 0, 0, W = DGROUP:0x90ea, H = DGROUP:0x10, BLACKNESS). Then seg10:0x6da6(0, 1), which saves working entries 10–245 to stored set 0 and blacks them ([Palette](#palette)). In state 9 with DGROUP:0x92be ≠ 0 both are skipped. [BYTES seg15:0x3071 seg15:0x3079-0x3094 seg15:0x3099-0x309d]
  2. **The slow-machine text,** only with DGROUP:0x93a6 = 0 and Calibrate DGROUP:0x8ffc < 850, or < 1600 with depth DGROUP:0x90ee < 8, all signed: "" (DGROUP:0x93e) + string 2137 "Incoming Jet " when W > 700 + string 2138 "Approaching Landing Pad..", drawn with seg10:0x94c4(0, 6, 0x0000FF00, W/2, DGROUP:0x90ec/2, text) on DGROUP:0x13dc before the scene. The scene's whole-screen BitBlt (step 6) then covers it: core-0008's trace draws it with 36 StretchBlts and no video frame shows it. With decision 9 of [ADR 0001](../decisions/0001-timestep.md) (Calibrate 14000) the port never draws it. [BYTES seg15:0x30af-0x3148] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 134] ids 2137–2138 [ORACLE core-0008]
  3. DGROUP:0x92d6 = trunc(22·H/100) and DGROUP:0x92d8 = H − 10 (state 9 with DGROUP:0x92be = 0: H/30 and 4H/10). [BYTES seg15:0x314b-0x318f]
  4. While the fast flag DGROUP:0x928 is set the routine jumps to seg15:0x3547, past the render, the blit and the frees. [BYTES seg15:0x3192 seg15:0x3199 seg15:0x3547-0x3554]
  5. It renders scene 105, the motherboard corridor, through ordinal 32 ([ispace.md](ispace.md#ordinal-notes), 32) at W × H with both override words and the Z delta 0, into the bitmap DGROUP:0x9294 in the DC DGROUP:0x929a, and sets DGROUP:0x92de = the out block's word +6 (slot 0's bottom + 1) and DGROUP:0x92d6 = its word +0xa − 0x28 (slot 1's top − 40). At 1024 × 768 that is DGROUP:0x92de = 629, DGROUP:0x92d6 = 161 and DGROUP:0x92d8 = 758. [BYTES seg15:0x31e0-0x320b seg15:0x3248-0x325a seg15:0x34f9] [BYTES IGRAPHIC.DLL 6600 105]
  6. BitBlt(DGROUP:0x13dc, 0, 0, W, H, DGROUP:0x929a, 0, 0, SRCCOPY), then the out block is unlocked and freed. This blit also runs in the states that render no scene; only the fast flag's jump skips it. [BYTES seg15:0x32fa-0x3301 seg15:0x33a6-0x33ad seg15:0x34fc-0x3541]

Then, only while depth ≥ 8 and DGROUP:0x93c ≠ 0, DGROUP:0x9004 is shifted left by 2 (Calibrate > 2000) or 1 (> 1500), and DGROUP:0x93c = 0, its only write; it is 1 in the DGROUP image, so this happens at the first landing of a session ([timing.md](timing.md#adaptive-screen-threshold)). The two allocation-failure exits seg15:0x31b5 and seg15:0x31d6 skip all of step 7. - **seg15:0x1a42, the jet's frames** (seg15:0x1a42–0x1eab, called only from seg15:0x3548). It calls the visit's ship builder seg15:0x20ca at seg15:0x1aaa except in state 9 with DGROUP:0x92be other than 0 and 2, then builds the incoming jet's frames; with the fast flag set seg15:0x1dab skips their drawing (seg15:0x1db2–0x1e11). Outside state 9, frame 0 is (DGROUP:0x92c6, DGROUP:0x92c8) at y 0. With (c, dc, q) = (350, 16, 4), (450, 27, 5), (550, 39, 6) and (650, 50, 7) for `Detail` 0 … 3 (another value leaves them unset), frames 1 … 9 get w = trunc(lo16(trunc(w0/q)·trunc(c/10))/10) and h = trunc(lo16(40w)/100), then c −= dc, where w0 is always frame 0's width. Frames 15, 14, … 10 get w = trunc(lo16(trunc(w0/q)·m)/10) and h = trunc(lo16(w·p)/100), with (m, p) going from (10, 100) by (+2, −12). Each frame's y (DGROUP:0x3ce8 + 2i) is the running sum of the heights stored before it. [BYTES seg15:0x1aad-0x1abc seg15:0x1ac2-0x1b2f seg15:0x1bf1-0x1c42 seg15:0x1c44-0x1ca1] The state-9 path (seg15:0x1aed-0x1be7) and the source stretch, DGROUP:0x92b2's square into DGROUP:0x3ccc, have an analyst's reading only (work/analysis/m4/s6-pad.md §2.6). seg15:0x1328(1) frees the frames at the descent's start. [BYTES seg15:0x1a42 seg15:0x1a94-0x1aaa seg15:0x1dab seg15:0x3548] [OPEN]

Frame f has the size (DGROUP:0x3d08 + 4f, DGROUP:0x3d0a + 4f) and the source y DGROUP:0x3ce8 + 2f in the image DC DGROUP:0x3cdc and the mask DC DGROUP:0x3cde. A step of 0 becomes 1. [BYTES seg15:0x3ce9-0x3d5a seg15:0x3d60-0x3d67]
  - **Phase 0,** each call: the index = (trunc(y10/−10) + DGROUP:0x92d8 − 55)/step, clamped to 0 … 15 (15 in phase 2; state 9 with DGROUP:0x92be ≥ 0 uses another formula). When the index grows, ordinal 14 runs through seg10:0x993a with (3, p) and (2, (p + 200)/3), p from the level's step, a relative fade. Outside state 9 the speed becomes max(150 − 4·index, 9). [BYTES seg15:0x3d7e-0x3de5 seg15:0x3da0-0x3dd1 seg15:0x3e47-0x3eb5 seg15:0x3f57-0x3f6a]

Phase 0 returns 0. **Phase 1:** the speed falls by 5 to at least 50 first, frame 15 is drawn (size [bp−0x18] = 15, source y DGROUP:0x3d06), then y10 += trunc(2·speed/20), and the call returns 1 once y10 > 10·(DGROUP:0x92d6 + 44). [BYTES seg15:0x42ac-0x42de seg15:0x42e1-0x42ef seg15:0x446d-0x44ec seg15:0x451f-0x453d]

The scene outside the work rectangle never changes: core-0003's fly-in frames c0039 … c0044 differ only inside x 419–604 at 32 bpp, so the SKY TALON's frame 0 is 186 px wide, and the ordinal-14 fades leave the drawn pixels alone. [BYTES seg15:0x3d2e-0x3d41 seg15:0x42ac-0x42de seg15:0x451f-0x453d] [ORACLE core-0003]

[ORACLE core-0008]
- **The descent's set-up.** seg15:0x3590's sub-step 0 calls seg15:0x3caa(0) on each frame; once it returns non-zero (state 9: DGROUP:0x92a2 = 2 and return) it clears the dword DGROUP:0x17f2, sets DGROUP:0x92a2 = 1, calls seg15:0x1328(1) and seg15:0x1eac, PatBlts the screen black with DGROUP:0x93a6 = 0, and runs ordinal 14 through seg10:0x993a with (3, 0xcc) and (2, 0x89) unless DGROUP:0x928 is set. Then, in state 6: seg10:0x6e76(−1, 1); seg10:0x6d7a(v, v, v) for v = 40, 44, … 224, 47 greys; seg10:0x6e4c(); seg10:0x6da6(1, 0); seg10:0x6e76(0, 2); the 47 greys again; seg10:0x6e4c(); the pad seg15:0x482e ([Game-start dialogs](#game-start-dialogs), item 7); seg10:0x6e76(−1, 1); seg10:0x6dd4(1). Frame i has (DGROUP:0x3d08, DGROUP:0x3d0a) + 4i = (s4, s4) and DGROUP:0x3ce8 + 2i = s4·i, so **frame 0 is the ship with its flame and frame 1 the ship without**. The descent's first call makes that buffer: DGROUP:0x9580 = CreateCompatibleDC(DGROUP:0x13dc) with DGROUP:0x9584 = CreateCompatibleBitmap(W × DGROUP:0x10), the palette, a BLACKNESS PatBlt, the pad rectangle DGROUP:0x3cb0 = (half(W) − half(padW), DGROUP:0x10 − pc − 0x34, + padW, + pc + 0x34) and the pad copied into it from DGROUP:0x92b6, then seg16:0x1f78, which calls seg16:0x27ea and seg16:0x2144. At its end (after the seg7:0x13b2 calls when DGROUP:0x93a6 ≠ 0 and 120 calls of seg7:0x158e) seg16:0x2144 BitBlts the whole buffer to the screen, restores and deletes it and zeroes both words, so the monitor scene appears in one blit; then, when DGROUP:0x952c ≠ 0, it calls the left monitor seg16:0x64fa. [BYTES seg15:0x379b-0x3859 seg16:0x2152-0x2161 seg16:0x271b-0x27d3 seg16:0x27d6-0x27de seg16:0x2052 seg16:0x2056]

With ry = DGROUP:0x6db6, rx = DGROUP:0x6db0, bh = DGROUP:0x6dae, vy = DGROUP:0x3ca2, edge = DGROUP:0x6dac + DGROUP:0x6db4, bottom = bh + ry, half = trunc(W/2), D = half − edge − 2 and N = half − 0x5a − edge, it computes y1 = (vy − ry)(D − 90)/D + ry and y2 = (vy − bottom)(D − 90)/D + bottom, 32-bit products of sign-extended words divided through the RTL's truncating seg5:0x110. Then, for i = 0, 2, … while i < N (signed 32-bit), it makes the grey c = lo16((−150·i)/N) + 170 as PALETTERGB from its low byte and PatBlts PATCOPY (edge + i, ry + i·(y1 − ry)/N, 2, bh + i·((y2 − bottom) − (y1 − ry))/N) and (DGROUP:0x6db0 − 2 − i, the same y, 2, that height + 2). Last, a 2-px black pen draws from (edge + 2, ry − 1), (edge + 2, bottom), (rx − 2, ry) and (rx − 2, bottom) to (half, vy), (half, vy + 2), (half, vy) and (half, vy). ## Window geometry
**Creation.** The main window's style, position and topmost flag at creation are under [Start-up](#start-up), step 15. [BYTES seg6:0x180e seg6:0x18a5 seg6:0x18d3]

**Show rectangle.** ShowLeft, ShowTop, ShowRight and ShowBottom live in DGROUP:0x1d20–0x1d26.
- When ShowRight ≤ 0 the start-up reader seg6:0x0 replaces the four Show values with a rectangle of 4/5 of the screen size, centred. It then moves a rectangle that reaches past the right screen edge left by the excess. One that reaches past the bottom loses twice the excess from ShowBottom while ShowTop stays, because seg6:0xfc and seg6:0x100 both add to DGROUP:0x1d26. [BYTES seg6:0x75 seg6:0x83 seg6:0x8a seg6:0xaf seg6:0xb1 seg6:0xc3 seg6:0xcd seg6:0xd7 seg6:0xe1 seg6:0xe7 seg6:0xeb seg6:0xf6 seg6:0xfc seg6:0x100]
- WM_MOVE (seg2:0x11c0) on a window that is not minimised stores GetWindowRect in the Show rectangle DGROUP:0x1d20 while Full = 0, and sets DGROUP:0x8f6a = 1 in both modes. seg6:0x33e writes the Show keys only while DGROUP:0x8f6a ≠ 0 and then clears it ([Exit](#exit)); the first WM_TIMER also clears it. [BYTES seg2:0x11ce seg2:0x11d7 seg2:0x11e5 seg2:0x11ea seg6:0x606 seg6:0x630 seg6:0x6a2 seg6:0x6be seg2:0x1211 seg2:0xb6e]

**Play area.** W = DGROUP:0x90ea is the play width, H = DGROUP:0x10 the client height, and DGROUP:0x90ec the play height above the HUD.
- **WM_SIZE.** The handler seg2:0xa74 ignores a minimised window. With Full (DGROUP:0x20) ≠ 0 and a window rectangle other than (0, 0, SM_CXSCREEN, SM_CYSCREEN) it calls MoveWindow(main, 0, 0, SM_CXSCREEN, SM_CYSCREEN, TRUE) and skips the size update. Otherwise it sets DGROUP:0x90ea = min(SM_CXSCREEN, cx), DGROUP:0x10 = min(SM_CYSCREEN, cy) (unsigned compares on the WM_SIZE client size) and DGROUP:0x90ec = DGROUP:0x10 − DGROUP:0xa70. [BYTES seg2:0xa85 seg2:0xab0 seg2:0xac5 seg2:0xaf5 seg2:0xafa seg2:0xb08 seg2:0xb15 seg2:0xb27 seg2:0xb34 seg2:0xb37 seg2:0xb3d]
  - On both paths (after the size update, and after the MoveWindow branch, which jumps from seg2:0xafa to seg2:0xb41 with the old sizes) WM_SIZE sets DGROUP:0x8e58 = 0 and DGROUP:0x8e5a = DGROUP:0x10 − 85. It rebuilds the clip region through seg2:0xca4, copies DGROUP:0x8f78 to DGROUP:0x8f76 and sets DGROUP:0x8f64 = 1 when DGROUP:0x1e ≠ 0. With Full = 0 it sets DGROUP:0x8f6a = 1 and stores GetWindowRect(main) in DGROUP:0x1d20. With DGROUP:0x9130 ≠ 0 and DGROUP:0x90f4 = 1 it calls seg18:0x270e with DGROUP:0x10 and DGROUP:0x90ea. [BYTES seg2:0xafa seg2:0xb41 seg2:0xb4d seg2:0xb51 seg2:0xb57 seg2:0xb61 seg2:0xb6e seg2:0xb7c seg2:0xb81 seg2:0xb88 seg2:0xb97]
- seg2:0x1ac8 (state change) and seg2:0xba4 (re-layout) take the size from GetClientRect: width = client right, or SM_CXSCREEN when that is not greater; height likewise with SM_CYSCREEN. seg2:0x1ac8 sets DGROUP:0x90ec = height − DGROUP:0xa70 and DGROUP:0x8e5a = height − 85. It replaces the clip region DGROUP:0x12 with CreateRectRgn(0, 0, DGROUP:0x90ea, DGROUP:0x90ec), without the border inset of seg2:0xca4. seg2:0xba4 sets DGROUP:0xa70 = 85 and calls seg2:0xca4. [BYTES seg2:0x1aea seg2:0x1af6 seg2:0x1b07 seg2:0x1b22 seg2:0x1b29 seg2:0x1b38 seg2:0x1b5d seg2:0xbc1 seg2:0xbfc seg2:0xc05 seg2:0xc52]
  - The re-layout seg2:0xba4 first PatBlts BLACKNESS around the player object (DGROUP:0x1e30 + DGROUP:0x9646·0x70, ±20 and +0x28 from its +0x22 size) and calls seg7:0x11cc and seg7:0x1208. It then blacks out the text-line rectangle DGROUP:0x1d28 (with Border) or the whole play area, and posts WM_PAINT. [BYTES seg2:0xc0c seg2:0xc33 seg2:0xc38 seg2:0xc3d seg2:0xc55 seg2:0xc86 seg2:0xc97]
- DGROUP:0xa70, the height of the HUD strip at the bottom of the client area, holds 85 in the file. Its only writer is seg2:0xbfc, which stores 85, and its readers are seg2:0xb3a and seg2:0x1b25. [BYTES seg2:0xbfc seg2:0xb3a seg2:0x1b25 DGROUP:0xa70]
- **Clip region.** seg2:0xca4 builds the play rectangle DGROUP:0x1d30 and the clip region DGROUP:0x12 = CreateRectRgn(L, T, W − L, DGROUP:0x90ec − m). PlayMode (DGROUP:0x9134) ≠ 0 adds 20 to m. [BYTES seg2:0xcb1 seg2:0xcbb seg2:0xcc8 seg2:0xcca seg2:0xcd2 seg2:0xcd7 seg2:0xce2 seg2:0xceb seg2:0xcf4 seg2:0xd03 seg2:0xd12 seg2:0xd1b seg2:0xd22 seg2:0xd54 seg2:0xd69]

  | Border (DGROUP:0xa78) | L | T | m | Text-line rectangle DGROUP:0x1d28..0x1d2e (x, y, cx, cy) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | ≠ 0 | 78 − (58 − W mod 58)/2 | 78 | 20 | (L, DGROUP:0x90ec − 40, W − 2L, 20) |
  | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | (0, DGROUP:0x10 − 105, W, 20) |

- DGROUP:0xa78 is INI Border (Game Border, default 1), stored by seg6:0xd51. When Border or Menu changes, the settings reader sets the menu, sets DGROUP:0x1038 = 1 and posts WM_PAINT; the next wave frame then calls the re-layout seg2:0xba4. seg11:0xafe draws the border as 58-pixel tiles with the same inset (58 − W mod 58)/2. [BYTES seg6:0xd20 seg6:0xd51 seg6:0xd86 seg6:0xd98 seg7:0x793 seg7:0x79a seg11:0xddd seg11:0xdef]
- **HUD.** seg11:0xafe lays out the wave HUD from the play size: the scrollbar (class "scrollbar", style 0x50000001, id 200) at (W/2 + 50, DGROUP:0x8e5a, 14, 85) when DGROUP:0x94e = 0, the IS_INFO panels id 210 at (DGROUP:0x93ba + 5, DGROUP:0x8e5a + 5, DGROUP:0x5024 − 9, 76) and id 220 at (DGROUP:0x93c4 + 5, DGROUP:0x8e5a + 5, DGROUP:0x5026 − 9, 76), and a PATCOPY fill of (0, DGROUP:0x8e5a, W, 85). The WM_PAINT routine seg2:0x970 calls seg11:0xafe while DGROUP:0x8f64 is set, which WM_SIZE sets, so a resize re-lays the HUD out at the new width and height. [BYTES seg11:0xca2 seg11:0xcb1 seg11:0xcbe seg11:0xcc2 seg11:0xcd9 seg11:0xd28 seg11:0xd36 seg11:0xd4e seg11:0xd79 seg11:0xd9f seg11:0xdcc seg2:0x9a9 seg2:0x9b0 seg2:0xb61]
  - seg2:0x970 sets DGROUP:0x8f64 itself in states 6 and 8, and in state 0 when DGROUP:0x95b8 is 4 or 5. When it finds DGROUP:0x8f64 = 0 it only sets it, without the HUD layout, so the HUD is laid out again from the next paint on. The writes that clear it are seg9:0x6649, seg9:0x6975, seg10:0x844a, seg10:0xa0c6 and seg11:0x3e5f. [BYTES seg2:0x98f seg2:0x9a3 seg2:0x9a9 seg2:0x9b7]

**Scale.**
- The visible world follows the client rectangle and the scale level. DGROUP:0x9150 holds 4 − Size, and the view extent in world units is W << (4 − Size) by DGROUP:0x90ec << (4 − Size). One pixel is 16, 8 or 4 world units at Size 0, 1 or 2. Nothing letterboxes or stretches the play area when the client size changes. [BYTES seg6:0xb07 seg9:0x2408 seg10:0x9b8d seg8:0x14 seg8:0x1b seg8:0x24 seg4:0x27d8 seg4:0x27e2 seg4:0x27f6]
- The scale level DGROUP:0x9156 (INI Size, default 1) takes the values 0, 1 and 2: seg10:0x9a2a beeps and changes nothing when Size + delta falls outside 0–2. The manual's 1/2x, 1x and 2x are levels 0, 1 and 2. [BYTES seg10:0x9a5f seg10:0x9a63 seg10:0x9a6a seg10:0x9a6e seg6:0xaf8]
- The writers of DGROUP:0x9156 are exactly seg6:0xafd, seg6:0x95ca, seg9:0x23bd, seg9:0x23fe, seg10:0x9ade, seg10:0x9b85, seg15:0x21c5, seg15:0x21d8, seg15:0x2bdc, seg15:0x2bf9, seg18:0x131, seg18:0x72d, seg18:0x800, seg18:0x903, seg18:0x910, seg18:0x2753 (shl by 1) and seg18:0x2ac3. [BYTES seg6:0xafd seg6:0x95ca seg9:0x23bd seg9:0x23fe seg10:0x9ade seg10:0x9b85 seg15:0x21c5 seg15:0x21d8 seg15:0x2bdc seg15:0x2bf9 seg18:0x131 seg18:0x72d seg18:0x800 seg18:0x903 seg18:0x910 seg18:0x2753 seg18:0x2ac3]
  - DGROUP:0x9150 is written only at seg6:0xb07, seg9:0x2408 and seg10:0x9b8d. The Size writes at seg6:0x95ca (saved-game header +0x171, which then sets DGROUP:0x92e2 and writes the INI), seg15:0x21c5/0x2bdc (temporarily 1) and in seg18 leave the view shift unchanged until one of those three runs. [BYTES seg6:0x95ca seg6:0x95ed seg15:0x21c5 seg15:0x21d8 seg18:0x2753 seg18:0x2ac3]
- **Rescale and keys.** The live Size change (Ctrl + / Ctrl −, the INI reread after the Options and New Game dialogs) is specified under [Rescale](#rescale-seg100x9a2a), [The image rescale](#the-image-rescale-seg100x6308) and [Size entry points](#size-entry-points).
  - Plain Plus or Minus (no Ctrl or Alt) moves the HUD scrollbar: DGROUP:0xa6c changes by ±8, clamped at 500 above, SetScrollPos(DGROUP:0xa7a, SB_CTL, 600 − value), then seg11:0x33cc runs. [BYTES seg11:0x720f seg11:0x7226 seg11:0x7233 seg11:0x7242 seg11:0x7247 seg11:0x7262]

**Mode switching.**
- **System commands.** seg2:0xd78 dispatches on wParam & 0xFFF0. Every unlisted code returns 1 and goes to DefWindowProc. [BYTES seg2:0xd85 seg2:0xd88 seg2:0xd90 seg2:0xd97 seg2:0xd9e seg2:0xda5 seg2:0xdac seg2:0xdb1 seg2:0xdb5 seg2:0xe6b seg2:0xe71 seg2:0xe74 seg2:0xe82 seg2:0xfa5 seg2:0xfb5 seg2:0xfba seg2:0x63f]

  | Code | Action | Returns |
  |---|---|---|
  | 0x60 "&Main Menus" | toggles the menu bar | whatever AX DrawMenuBar leaves |
  | 0xc0 | posts WM_COMMAND 0xc0 | 0 |
  | SC_MINIMIZE | pauses through seg2:0x9c4 | 1, so DefWindowProc minimizes |
  | SC_MAXIMIZE | seg2:0xfc4 when DGROUP:0x1e is set | 1, so DefWindowProc also maximizes |
  | SC_RESTORE | restores the windowed rectangle (below) | 1, so DefWindowProc also restores |
  | SC_CLOSE | the exit sequence ([Exit](#exit)) | 0 |
  | any other | none | 1 |

- **SC_RESTORE** sets DGROUP:0x8f68 = 1 and moves the window to the ShowLeft–ShowBottom rectangle. The first time (DGROUP:0x14) it appends a separator and "&Main Menus" (string 2184, id 0x60, checked when Menu is non-zero) to the system menu. It returns to full screen through seg2:0xfc4 when Full is non-zero, and sets the caption to string 2337 while DGROUP:0x8f66 is set. [BYTES seg2:0xe87 seg2:0xe8b seg2:0xea6 seg2:0xeaa seg2:0xeaf seg2:0xebc seg2:0xec1 seg2:0xece seg2:0xed7 seg2:0xef3 seg2:0xf07 seg2:0xf15 seg2:0xf1d seg2:0xf20 seg2:0xf45] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 137] id 2184 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 147] id 2337
- **Command 0x60** flips INI Menu (DGROUP:0x90f6) between 0 and 1, attaches DGROUP:0x90fa or no menu with SetWindowWord(main, GWW_ID, …), checks or unchecks item 0x60 and calls DrawMenuBar. [BYTES seg2:0xf4d seg2:0xf5b seg2:0xf64 seg2:0xf75 seg2:0xf8b seg2:0xf97 seg2:0xfa0]
- **Full screen.** seg2:0xfc4 sets DGROUP:0x20 = 1, ANDs the style with 0xFF30FFFF (clearing WS_CAPTION, WS_SYSMENU, WS_THICKFRAME and both boxes) and calls MoveWindow(main, 0, 0, SM_CXSCREEN, SM_CYSCREEN, TRUE). It makes the window HWND_TOPMOST when OnTop is non-zero. [BYTES seg2:0xfd1 seg2:0xfdd seg2:0xfeb seg2:0xff3 seg2:0x1012 seg2:0x101d seg2:0x1039]
- **Double click.** A double click outside the three HUD hotspots toggles the mode once DGROUP:0x1e is set. From full screen it ORs 0x00CF0000 into the style, calls SetWindowPos(main, HWND_NOTOPMOST, 0, 0, 0, 0, SWP_NOSIZE\|SWP_NOMOVE), posts SC_RESTORE and sets DGROUP:0x20 = 0; the SC_RESTORE handler then moves the window to the Show rectangle. From windowed mode it calls seg2:0xfc4. WndProc reaches this only when DGROUP:0x1212 and DGROUP:0x22 are set. [BYTES seg2:0x1046 seg2:0x1152 seg2:0x1159 seg2:0x1177 seg2:0x117f seg2:0x1188 seg2:0x1194 seg2:0x119d seg2:0x11a7 seg2:0x11ac seg2:0x11b5 seg2:0x65e-0x66a]
- **Boss key.** WM_COMMAND 0x19c (Alt+B) ORs 0x00CF0000 into the style only while Full is non-zero and leaves DGROUP:0x20 at 1, then posts SC_MINIMIZE, which pauses and minimizes. The later SC_RESTORE therefore first moves the window to the Show rectangle and then goes back to full screen through seg2:0xfc4. [BYTES seg2:0x1486 seg2:0x14a4 seg2:0x14ac seg2:0x14b5 seg2:0xe70 seg2:0xe8b seg2:0xf15 seg2:0xf1d]
- **Full and DGROUP:0x22.** DGROUP:0x20 (Full) is written only at seg6:0x1a (INI), seg2:0xfd1 (1) and seg2:0x11ac (0). DGROUP:0x22 starts as 1 in the initialised data. It is written only at seg7:0x19 (1 on each tick pass while DGROUP:0x1a is 0) and at seg7:0xff, seg7:0x197, seg7:0x1bf and seg7:0x211 (0 in states 6, 8 and 9 and in state 0 while DGROUP:0x9294 is set). Its only readers are seg2:0x3eb (WM_GETMINMAXINFO) and seg2:0x665 (WM_LBUTTONDBLCLK), so in those states the window keeps its size and a double click cannot switch modes. [BYTES seg6:0x1a seg2:0xfd1 seg2:0x11ac DGROUP:0x22 seg7:0x10 seg7:0x19 seg7:0xff seg7:0x197 seg7:0x1bf seg7:0x20a seg7:0x211 seg2:0x3eb seg2:0x665]

**Watch windows.** IconWnd (DGROUP:0x8f72) and SmWnd (DGROUP:0x8f74) record whether the IS_ICON window (procedure seg7:0x6184) and the IS_SMART window (seg13:0x613a) are shown; their SC_CLOSE clears the key. VScale (DGROUP:0xb6e) and SScale (DGROUP:0x95f8) are the checks of each window's system-menu item 0xF0 "1/2x Scale". The window's WM_GETMINMAXINFO fixes its width to 50 >> scale and its height to SM_CYCAPTION + n·46 + 6 (n·23 + 3 at scale 1), with n = DGROUP:0x91fe or DGROUP:0x95fa. None of the four changes the main play area. [BYTES seg2:0x4f7 seg2:0x505 seg2:0x51a seg2:0x528 seg7:0x61e9 seg7:0x6233 seg7:0x627f seg7:0x62d5 seg7:0x62e1 seg7:0x62fd seg7:0x631c seg13:0x61ea seg13:0x6237 seg13:0x628d seg13:0x62b5 seg13:0x62d4 seg6:0x156c seg6:0x15c5]

**Colour depth.** The title screen draws "For best results, change Windows to 256 color mode" unless DGROUP:0x90ee is exactly 8 (step 13 of [Start-up](#start-up)). [BYTES seg15:0x71e8 seg15:0x721d]

### Rescale (seg10:0x9a2a)
seg10:0x9a2a(delta) (cdecl, one argument, ±1) is a relative rescale: it sets Size to delta + 1 (2 or 0) so that seg10:0x6308 doubles or halves every image slot, then sets the new Size, doubles or halves the size words of every object and rebuilds what must render afresh. Callers: the key switch ([Size entry points](#size-entry-points)), seg10:0x7ff4, seg10:0x8008 and seg10:0x800f (inside seg10:0x7f88). [BYTES seg11:0x7207 seg10:0x7ff4 seg10:0x8008 seg10:0x800f]

**Gate, range, dialog.**
- Outside state 1 (DGROUP:0x90f4 ≠ 1) or in seg18 mode (DGROUP:0x9130 ≠ 0) it stores DGROUP:0x103c = 1 and returns through the epilogue that skips the DGROUP:0x931e restore. It writes no INI value, and the next wave frame's seg10:0x7f88 compares INI `Size` with DGROUP:0x9156, which the press changed neither of, so a press outside state 1 has no later effect: the flag only replays an INI difference that already existed. A port must not queue the delta. [BYTES seg10:0x9a39-0x9a4d seg10:0xa10e seg7:0xb73 seg10:0x7fa9-0x7fb4]
- It saves DGROUP:0x931e (`Quick`) and clears it for the whole pass. When delta + DGROUP:0x9156 is above 2 or below 0 it calls MessageBeep(0) and returns past the restore, so the beep leaves `Quick` = 0 and every later sheet render takes the full renderer (`sel` = 0) until seg6:0xb3e next runs ([globals.md](globals.md#global-table), DGROUP:0x931e). The port keeps this fault; it does not depend on the CPU. [BYTES seg10:0x9a50-0x9a73 seg10:0xa108-0xa10e]
- With DGROUP:0x934c = 0 it sets DGROUP:0x9518 = 1 and stores in DGROUP:0x934c the modeless CreateDialog(DGROUP:0x9212, MAKEINTRESOURCE(101), DGROUP:0x16, seg9:0x90fe). The procedure's WM_INITDIALOG puts string 2269 "Resizing graphics...Please hold on." into control 114 and centres the dialog on the screen. Nothing pumps messages before the pass ends, so the dialog gets no WM_PAINT during it. [BYTES seg10:0x9a76-0x9a9a] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL DIALOG 101] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 142] id 2269
- The reload flag, the local [bp − 0x14], is 1 exactly when delta + Size = 1 and either delta = 1, or delta = −1 with DGROUP:0x8fe8 (`Enhance`) ≠ 0 and DGROUP:0x90f0 = 0 (a display below 8 bpp). The pass branches on the local; only the store of 1 to DGROUP:0x103a is shared with it ([globals.md](globals.md#global-table)). [BYTES seg10:0x9a9d-0x9ad5 seg10:0x9ab6]

**Images** (temp Size = delta + 1, stored in DGROUP:0x9156 at seg10:0x9ada).
- Without reload it calls seg10:0x6308(k, −1, −1, −2) for k = 0 … 0x39 except 0x20 (the unscaled icon backup). Its extra skip for 0x27 < k < 0x30 at temp Size 2 needs the reload flag and never applies. The fourth argument −2 is not 2, so temp Size is never 1 and the Size-1 return is never forced. [BYTES seg10:0x9ae7-0x9b26 seg10:0x9b16]
- With reload it calls the same only for k = 14 … DGROUP:0x92ec + 13 (the icon flame images). On both paths each non-zero word DGROUP:0x4e94 + 2i, i < DGROUP:0x9408 (the fragment bitmaps), becomes seg10:0x6308(−1, −1, that word, −1). [BYTES seg10:0x9b28-0x9b4b seg10:0x9b4d-0x9b80]
- It then stores DGROUP:0x9156 = delta + old Size and DGROUP:0x9150 = 4 − Size, frees slot 0x22 with seg10:0x552(0x22, 1) and calls seg10:0x5c42, which rebuilds the flame cell f = (9 << Size) sar 1 (6·Size + 3 in seg18 mode): it frees DGROUP:0x3c60 through seg10:0x5cfa when non-zero, stores DGROUP:0x3c60 = seg10:0x7032(0, 0x10, 0) and, only when slot 0x22's DC DGROUP:0x3a2c is 0, makes a 16f × 16f compatible bitmap DGROUP:0x3b14 blacked with BLACKNESS. f is a local; it is 18 at Size 2 and 4 at Size 0. [BYTES seg10:0x9b82-0x9b9d seg10:0x5c4f-0x5cf2 seg10:0x5c56 seg10:0x5c64 seg10:0x5c79]

**Object size words.** For each pool p = DGROUP:0x9326 … DGROUP:0x9324 and each record below the allocated count DGROUP:0x92ec + 2p: [BYTES seg10:0x9ba0-0x9cb5]
- At new Size 0 a ship-pool record with an odd +0x22 first gets +0x22 + 1 and +0x24 − 1 (33/5 becomes 34/4 and then 17/2), as seg14:0x0's rule does. [BYTES seg10:0x9bbc-0x9bec seg14:0xbb5-0xbc5]
- Then +0x22 = lo16(+0x22 << (delta + 1)) sar 1, +0x24 likewise, and at the end +0x12 = +0x24. A pool-3 record whose scaled +0x22 is below 12 << new Size gets +0x24 = 4 when delta = 1 and new Size > 0, and also when new Size = 1 with DGROUP:0x8fe8 ≠ 0 and DGROUP:0x90f0 = 0. So small rocks (size 22 at Size 1) become 44/4 at Size 2 (the pitch 48 of the unscaled big-rock image that slots 8 and 9 then hold), 22/2 at 2 → 1 and 22/4 by the reload path. The range starts at DGROUP:0x9326, which is 1 in a Demon's Gate wave, so pool 0 keeps its size words there. `extent` +0x20 is untouched except for pool 8 below. [BYTES seg10:0x9bf0-0x9c1f seg10:0x9c23-0x9c4b seg10:0x9c51-0x9c8b seg10:0x9c91-0x9ca5]

**The ship block**, only when the reload flag is 1 or (new Size = 2 and DGROUP:0x8fe8 ≠ 0). [BYTES seg10:0x9cbf-0x9cd3]
1. seg10:0x552(k, 1) for k = 0x30 … 0x39 (the weapon images and the fruit). [BYTES seg10:0x9cd7-0x9cf0]
2. For each ship i ≤ DGROUP:0x9316: seg10:0x552(0x28 + i, 1); ship bytes +0xac and +0xad are read (sign-extended) before the render; "Loading Ship # " + i is built in a local and shown nowhere; seg14:0x11a8(i) renders the sheet at the new Size with `Quick` 0 (at Size 2 ordinal 2 renders 2× itself, so seg10:0x6308 is skipped, [ispace.md](ispace.md)). The side and gap are roster bytes +0x7a and +0x7b (sign-extended; roster record +0x7c·word +0xd6 of far DGROUP:0x6e24) when ship word +0xd6 ≠ −1, else the object's +0x22/+0x24 as seg14:0x11a8 left them. The side goes to design word +0x2a (DGROUP:0x96fc + 0xad·n, n = ship word +0x4e), then the object gets +0x22 = (side << Size) sar 1, +0x24 = (gap << Size) sar 1 and +0x12 = +0x24. DGROUP:0x92e4 = 1 is set before those stores and cleared after the wing cuts: seg8:0x2cd8(i, 0) when +0xac was 0 and seg8:0x2cd8(i, 1) when +0xad was 0 (no taunts, no debris, no sound; they still set ship dword +0x156 = now − 10000 and recompute design word +0x3c at the new Size). [BYTES seg10:0x9cf2-0x9e43 seg10:0x9db8-0x9dd6 seg10:0x9d28 seg10:0x9d7f-0x9da5 seg10:0x9dde-0x9e0d seg10:0x9e0d-0x9e15 seg10:0x9cfa-0x9d7b seg8:0x2d96]
3. For k = 0x17 … 0x1b: seg10:0x552(k, 1) and the words DGROUP:0x381a, 0x381c and 0x381e + 6k = 0xffff (the hazard image cache). [BYTES seg10:0x9e46-0x9e72 seg10:0x9e5b]
4. For each pool-8 entry j < DGROUP:0x9416 with word DGROUP:0x3d6c + 0x34j = 2: class (word DGROUP:0x3d80 + 0x34j) ≤ 2 (signed) sets the word to 1; classes 3 and 4 are left alone; class ≥ 5 runs seg10:0x18b0(word 0x3d72, word 0x3d70, word 0x3d7a, m, class) with m = 1 when byte DGROUP:0x4d93 + 0x14·class = 0xff (before the size stores, so it sees the scaled sizes), then sets record j's +0x24 = (sext byte DGROUP:0x4d99 + 0x14·class << Size) sar 1, +0x22 likewise from DGROUP:0x4d98 and +0x20 = lo16(+0x22 << 3), then seg10:0x533a(word 0x3d5a) when that word > 0 and word DGROUP:0x2f5e + 0x50·(that word) ≠ 0, and last stores seg10:0x18b0's result in word 0x3d6e. The placement's template stores +0x20 = sext(byte) << 3 without Size, so after a live rescale a class ≥ 5 hazard's `extent` differs from a fresh load's at Size 0 and 2 (it equals it only at Size 1). The template seg9:0x1a42-0x1cb3 serves every class of the table-word-2 path, so classes 3 and 4 keep +0x20 = b << 3. Both seg10:0x18b0 and seg10:0x533a read `Quick`, which is 0 here. Whether a kind-2 record can have class ≤ 2 is [OPEN] (M5). [BYTES seg10:0x9e74-0x9f5c seg10:0x9e89-0x9f33 seg9:0x1c18-0x1cb3 seg9:0x1c9b-0x1caa seg10:0x9f09 seg10:0x9f56 seg10:0x9e80]
5. Whether seg14:0x11a8's first shared branch (ship word +0x50 ≠ i and the ship it names has bytes +0xac and +0xad both set, seg14:0x11cf-0x1206) leaves +0x22/+0x24 as item 2 reads them, so that a ship with +0xd6 = −1 scales its already scaled sizes twice, depends on seg10:0x86e2, which that branch calls when design word +0x46 ≠ 0; whether it rewrites them is [OPEN]. [BYTES seg14:0x11cf-0x1206 seg14:0x1209-0x123c seg14:0x12d8-0x134d]

**The reload path** (reload flag 1, after the images): [BYTES seg10:0x9f5e-0x9fd7]
1. seg10:0x9568 frees with force 1 and rebuilds, in order: slots 1 and 2 (seg10:0x3ea2, gate posts); 0x16 (seg10:0x58ce(1)); 4 (seg10:0x565a); 0xc (seg10:0x5202); 5 (seg10:0x39a8(1)); 6, 8, 7 and 9 (seg10:0x3a2e, which clears DGROUP:0x740); 0xa and 0xb (seg10:0x5102); 0x1d (seg10:0x42ee); 3 (seg10:0x561a) and 0 (seg10:0x464a, BITMAP "DEF", pool 2). It then frees slot 0x1e on every call and calls seg10:0x49d4 only when DGROUP:0x9320 = 6. Every free is seg10:0x552(slot, 1). [BYTES seg10:0x9572-0x9668 seg10:0x9654-0x965d seg10:0x9660-0x9668]
2. seg10:0x552(0xd, 1) and seg10:0x5b30 rebuild the icon strip unscaled, then for i < DGROUP:0x92ec a BitBlt SRCCOPY of the 32 × 32 cell i of DGROUP:0x3a28 at (32i, 0) to DGROUP:0x3a02 at (38i + 6, 6). [BYTES seg10:0x9f7f-0x9fa3 seg10:0x9f83]
3. seg9:0x94ba paints the overlays: icon byte +0x2f = 0x9c for a directory byte +0x1c8 + k of 2 (ice), seg7:0x2512(k, byte − 1) for each icon whose byte is non-zero, and in a kind-2 wave seg7:0x2512(k, 0) when DGROUP:0x9334 = 0 or, with DGROUP:0x9334 ≠ 0, when `rand()` % 3 = 0. A reload rescale in a kind-2 wave with DGROUP:0x9334 ≠ 0 therefore draws 8 `rand()`, one per icon k = 0 … 7 at seg9:0x952c in ascending order, and skipping seg9:0x94ba moves the RNG stream ([rng.md](rng.md)). Then seg7:0x233c(0, i, 0) runs for each i < DGROUP:0x92ec; with its third argument 0 it plays no sound and draws no `rand()`. These are the only `rand()` the pass can draw: seg3:0x6ec needs DGROUP:0x92e4 = 0, and seg13:0x54da needs the ship pool. [BYTES seg9:0x94cd-0x954f seg9:0x94e2-0x9545 seg7:0x23ea seg7:0x24a6 seg3:0x6ec seg13:0x54da]

**Design words and the end.** For each ship i ≤ DGROUP:0x9316 (n = design_index): side = design +0x2a, plus 1 at Size 0 when odd; design words +0x00 and +0x02 = (2·(side sar Size))/2; +0x04 = 4 − (+0x04) − side/2 (side/2 by cwd/sub/sar) only when +0x04 < 0; then seg10:0x1502(n) and seg10:0x5d3c(i). The words +0x00 and +0x02 are read only at seg10:0x147b and seg10:0x163b, whose callers seg14:0xcdc, seg14:0xce6, seg16:0x610f and seg16:0x6118 follow a rewrite of the same design n in straight-line code (seg14:0xc4a-0xc54, seg16:0x60e2-0x60eb), so the rescale's values are never read. The local [bp − 0xc] (6, 5 or 4) is never read. [BYTES seg10:0x9fd9-0xa078 seg10:0xa001-0xa008 seg10:0xa015 seg10:0xa019 seg10:0x147b seg10:0x163b seg14:0xc50 seg14:0xc54 seg14:0xcdc seg14:0xce6 seg16:0x60e7 seg16:0x60eb seg16:0x610f seg16:0x6118 seg10:0x1502 seg10:0x5d3c seg10:0x9fe0 seg10:0x9ff5 seg10:0xa073 seg10:0xa02c]
- It writes INI `Size` (section DGROUP:0x9690, file DGROUP:0x96a9, the key text at DGROUP:0x908) and, when DGROUP:0x95bc ≠ 0, `SetSize` (DGROUP:0x90d); a demo writes `Size` only. When DGROUP:0x934c ≠ 0 it clears DGROUP:0x8f64, destroys the dialog, clears DGROUP:0x9518 and DGROUP:0x934c and posts 0x978 to DGROUP:0x16 (seg2:0x970 then skips one repaint and sets DGROUP:0x8f64, [world.md](world.md#update-loop)); the WM_PAINT the destroyed dialog leaves runs the full HUD repaint. It ends with PatBlt BLACKNESS of (0, 0, DGROUP:0x90ea, DGROUP:0x90ec) on DGROUP:0x13dc and restores DGROUP:0x931e. [BYTES seg10:0xa07b-0xa10b seg10:0xa0df seg10:0xa101 seg10:0xa0a2]

**What each transition rebuilds** (`Enhance` default 1, depth ≥ 8 on a modern display):

| Transition | Images | Objects | Ships | Hazards (M5) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 → 2 | slots 0 … 0x39 except 0x20 doubled; 8 and 9 take 6 and 7's unscaled images, DGROUP:0x740 = 1; fragments doubled; slot 0x22 and DGROUP:0x3c60 rebuilt at f = 18 | +0x22/+0x24 doubled, small rocks gap 4 | with `Enhance`: sheets re-rendered at 2×, wings re-cut, weapon images rebuilt; without it the doubled sheets stay | with `Enhance`: slots 0x17 … 0x1b rebuilt for class ≥ 5, extent doubled |
| 2 → 1 with depth ≥ 8 or no `Enhance` | every slot halved (6 and 7 back to 1×, 8 and 9 the 1× big rock halved, small-rock gap 2 while DGROUP:0x740 is set) | halved | none: the 2× sheets are halved | none |
| 2 → 1 with depth < 8 and `Enhance` | the reload path | halved, small rocks gap 4 | re-rendered at 1×, wings re-cut | rebuilt |
| 1 → 0 | every slot halved | halved, odd ship side + 1 first | none | none |
| 0 → 1 | icon flame images doubled, then the reload path | doubled, small rocks gap 4 | re-rendered at 1×, wings re-cut | rebuilt |
| outside state 1 or seg18 mode | nothing, DGROUP:0x103c = 1 | | | |
| beyond 0 … 2 | MessageBeep(0), `Quick` left 0 | | | |

DGROUP:0x740 outlives the rescale: after 1 → 2, and after 2 → 1 without the reload, it stays 1, and new pool-3 placements at Size 1 get a gap of 2 instead of 4 (seg10:0xb94 at seg10:0xe20-0xe7c; seg7:0x5090 at seg7:0x54ed-0x55a3 sets the pool DGROUP:0x934a record's +0x24 = +0x12 = 4 at Size 2 with the flag set, 2 at Size 1 and 1 at Size 0, the last two when the flag is set or DGROUP:0x9130 ≠ 0). [BYTES seg10:0x638f seg10:0x63bf seg10:0x3aa1 seg10:0xe4b seg10:0xe67 seg7:0x54f4 seg7:0x552c seg7:0x556b]

**Seen in the original.** [ORACLE core-0011]
- Ctrl + the OEM plus key or Ctrl + KP_Add steps Size up by one and Ctrl + minus or Ctrl + KP_Subtract steps it down by one; Ctrl + minus at Size 0 and Ctrl + plus at Size 2 change nothing, the plus and minus keys without Ctrl and Alt + plus change no Size, and a press rewrites INI `Size` and `SetSize` within 1.2 s. [ORACLE core-0011]
- The Size word changes about 25 ms after the key event. The first video frame at 60 fps that shows the new scale follows 17 – 50 ms later, no video frame is longer than 17 ms, and the whole pass fits in one or two video frames. [ORACLE core-0011]
- Each press redraws the play area in one frame (3 000 – 6 500 changed pixels) and the HUD strip in the same frame: 4 500 pixels for 1 → 0, 0 → 1 and 2 → 1, 31 600 pixels for 1 → 2 across the strip's whole width. In 9 of 24 presses one video frame has no non-black pixel left of the icon column. What the 31 600 pixels are, and which presses show the black frame, are not decoded. [ORACLE core-0011] [OPEN]
- The ship's sprite box at Sizes 0, 1 and 2 is 15 × 11, 31 × 23 and 62 × 46 pixels (TRANSFORMER) and 15 × 13, 31 × 28 and 62 × 56 (SKY TALON), with the sprite's top-left cell origin at the same screen point at every Size; the Size 2 picture has more detail than a doubling of the Size 1 picture. [ORACLE core-0011]
- The launched missile's box is 7 × 11, 13 × 23 and 32 × 44 pixels at Sizes 0, 1 and 2; a gun shot is five 2 × 2 dots at Size 1 and five 4 × 4 dots at Size 2; the ring of death's extent is 88, 182 and 364 pixels across and its fuel cost does not depend on Size; the HUD strip keeps its size and place at all three Sizes. [ORACLE core-0011]

### The image rescale (seg10:0x6308)
seg10:0x6308(slot, a, hbm, mode) (cdecl): [bp + 6] slot, [bp + 8] never read, [bp + 0xa] a bitmap handle (read only for slot −1), [bp + 0xc] mode. DC[k] = DGROUP:0x39e8 + 2k, BMP[k] = DGROUP:0x3ad0 + 2k, OLD[k] = DGROUP:0x3974 + 2k, PAL[k] = DGROUP:0x3a5c + 2k. [BYTES seg10:0x6316-0x664b]
1. A slot ≥ 0 whose DC is 0 returns 0 (the test applies to every slot ≠ −1). At DGROUP:0x9156 = 1 with mode ≠ 2 it returns hbm unchanged, before the slot's own bitmap replaces the local hbm, so a slot call at Size 1 returns the −1 passed in. A slot call then replaces hbm by BMP[slot]. [BYTES seg10:0x6316-0x633d seg10:0x6334 seg10:0x6340-0x634f]
2. At Size 2: slots 8 and 9 return 0 untouched. Slot 7 calls seg10:0x552(9, 1) and copies DC, BMP, OLD and PAL of slot 7 into slot 9, slot 6 the same into 8 (seg10:0x552(8, 1)); both store DGROUP:0x740 = 1. For slots 6 and 7 at Size 2 the old DC only gets its saved object back (no palette restore, no DeleteDC), and at the end SelectObject(DC[9 or 8], BMP[9 or 8]) → OLD[9 or 8] replaces DeleteObject. [BYTES seg10:0x6352-0x6363 seg10:0x6359 seg10:0x635f seg10:0x638f seg10:0x63bf seg10:0x647b-0x648d seg10:0x65ce-0x65f9]
3. GetObject(hbm, 14, …) gives w and h; the new bitmap is W = lo16(w << Size) sar 1 by H = lo16(h << Size) sar 1 (Size 2: 2w × 2h; Size 0: floor(w/2) × floor(h/2), so the 869 × 53 rock images become 434 × 26), made with CreateCompatibleBitmap of the old slot DC while the old bitmap is still selected (the new bitmap takes its format; the monochrome slots 6 and 8 stay monochrome), or of DGROUP:0x13dc for slot −1, and blacked with PatBlt BLACKNESS (rop 0x42) once selected into the new DC. Both DCs are CreateCompatibleDC of the slot DC (DGROUP:0x13dc for slot −1). The only DGROUP:0x13dc uses are those three (seg10:0x648f, 0x649b, 0x64a7), so the routine draws nothing on the window, which answers the question for seg10:0x565a's call (slot 4). [BYTES seg10:0x63c5-0x641f seg10:0x6407-0x641f seg10:0x6513-0x6532 seg10:0x63c8 seg10:0x648f-0x64c4 seg10:0x6505-0x6510 seg10:0x649b seg10:0x64a7]
4. SetStretchBltMode(new DC, 2 for slot 8 or 6, else 3) and seg10:0x9962(1, newDC, 0, 0, W, H, srcDC, 0, 0, w, h, 0x00CC0020). Mode 2 (WHITEONBLACK) ORs the pixels a shrink drops, so a halved mask keeps every set pixel; a 2× copy duplicates in every mode. [BYTES seg10:0x6537-0x654f seg10:0x6546 seg10:0x654d seg10:0x6554-0x6587]
5. The old bitmap is deleted and the source DC released. Saved palettes are written only when DGROUP:0x920e ≠ 0, but [bp − 0x10] is stored into PAL[slot] regardless and seg10:0x552 tests only that word before SelectPalette, so with DGROUP:0x920e = 0 the stored word is stale stack ([ADR 0016](../decisions/0016-undefined-reads.md): store 0). Apart from slots 6 and 7 at Size 2 the old DC gets SelectObject(OLD[slot]), SelectPalette(PAL[slot]) when DGROUP:0x920e ≠ 0 and PAL[slot] ≠ 0, and DeleteDC. A slot returns 0 after storing DC, BMP, OLD and PAL; slot −1 returns the new bitmap (callers: seg7:0x23ff, seg7:0x2577, seg7:0x29e9, seg7:0x47d8, seg10:0x708f, seg10:0x9b73, seg12:0x1b94, seg13:0x469b, seg13:0x475e) and puts the new DC's palette back with SelectObject, not SelectPalette (seg10:0x663e: Win16 SelectObject fails on a palette, and the DC is deleted at once, so the return is unread). [BYTES seg10:0x64c7-0x64f4 seg10:0x661c seg10:0x663e seg10:0x5c2-0x5d3 seg10:0x65c4 seg10:0x65fc-0x6623 seg10:0x6626-0x664b]
6. `mode` is compared only with 2, so −1 and −2 act alike. [BYTES seg10:0x6334]

**seg10:0x9962(unused, dst, x, y, W, H, src, sx, sy, w, h, rop)** (cdecl; [bp + 6] is never read) measures the source depth with CreateCompatibleBitmap(src, 2, 2) and GetObject (planes × bits per pixel). It calls ISPACE.DLL ordinal 36 through seg10:0x6bca when DGROUP:0x742 ≠ 0, the depth is neither 1 nor above 8, and (W = 2w and H = 2h) or W < 0 or H < 0; otherwise StretchBlt(dst, x, y, W, H, src, sx, sy, w, h, rop). So on a 16/24/32-bpp display every rescale is a GDI StretchBlt; at 2 – 8 bpp every doubling meets the 2× test (W = 2w for w < 0x4000) and goes to ordinal 36 (its plain 2× result is [OPEN]); a halving and a monochrome source always take StretchBlt. [BYTES seg10:0x9974-0x99a0 seg10:0x99ad seg10:0x99ba-0x99d8 seg10:0x6bca seg10:0x9977 seg10:0x999b seg6:0xcd1-0xcdf]

**Every call of seg10:0x6308.** The slot −1 calls store their result: seg7:0x23f7 (CRACKS, from seg7:0x233c for amount ≠ 0), seg7:0x256f (graphic 360, ice and virus), seg7:0x29e1 ("piece"), seg7:0x47d0 (graphic 230, flame cells), seg12:0x1b8c (OUT_SIGN, the exit sign, on every flash with the source x scaled), seg13:0x4693 and seg13:0x4756 (graphic 230), seg10:0x7089 (the flame mirror of seg10:0x7032, argument [bp − 4]), seg10:0x1d9d (the hazard slots 0x17 … 0x1b, with ([bp − 0x10], 1, 1, 1)). The slot calls (k, −1, −1, −1) are seg9:0x5dae (slot 0xd), seg10:0x3a24 (5, when seg10:0x39a8's argument ≠ 0), seg10:0x3e65 … 0x3e92 (7, 6, 9, 8), seg10:0x42cf and 0x42de (1, 2), seg10:0x4407 (0x1d), seg10:0x463a (the anim-4 sheet), seg10:0x49c5 (slot 0 from seg10:0x4672), seg10:0x4b24 (0x1e), seg10:0x51c3 (10, 11), seg10:0x532b (0xc), seg10:0x58c4 (4), seg10:0x5b26 (0x16), seg10:0x6fde (the slot of seg10:0x6ee6 when [bp + 0x20] ≠ 0: the ship, weapon and hook sheets), seg10:0x9b20 and seg10:0x9b39 (every slot, fourth argument −2) and seg11:0x9347 (the fruit, slot DGROUP:0x37c0). The fourth argument of the six slot −1 sites whose window starts after it is the `push −1` before the inner call.
